# Project Agent Rules

- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.

- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

# Stream1 MLP Native vs PyTorch Kaggle 2xT4 - 2026-07-01

Kaggle kernel: `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-mlp-native-vs-torch-2xt4-benchmark`, version 2.

Source tag: `stream1-mlp-torch-kaggle-v2-code` at `cc90a3d`.

Status: `KernelWorkerStatus.COMPLETE`.

## Best Per-GPU Throughput

| GPU | Native B_MICRO | Native concurrency | Native candidates/s | Torch rows | Torch candidates/s | Native / Torch |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 4096 | 1 | 16,416,335.8 | 16,384 | 8,133,621.3 | 2.0183x |
| 1 | 4096 | 1 | 15,728,371.2 | 4,096 | 6,763,818.6 | 2.3254x |

Aggregate best throughput across both T4 GPUs:

- Native CUTLASS MLP: 32,144,707.0 candidates/s.
- PyTorch preindexed MLP: 14,897,439.9 candidates/s.
- Native / Torch aggregate: 2.1577x.

## Evidence Files

Downloaded Kaggle outputs are under:

- `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_benchmark_v2_output/mlp_native_vs_torch_comparison.csv`
- `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_benchmark_v2_output/native_mlp_benchmark_rows.csv`
- `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_benchmark_v2_output/torch_mlp_benchmark_rows.csv`
- `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_benchmark_v2_output/cayley-beam-mlp-native-vs-torch-2xt4-benchmark.log`

## Interpretation

For the exported folded MLP weights, the native CUTLASS Stream1 path is about 2.0-2.3x faster than a direct PyTorch implementation on Kaggle T4. This establishes the local comparison baseline for judging the transformer backend: the transformer PyTorch fast path being faster than native means the transformer native path still has implementation overhead, not that native CUDA is inherently slower than Torch for this project.

# Stream1 Piece Transformer Clean Tensor Attention - 2026-07-01

## Change

Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path away from the previous pointer-array `GemmArray` implementation.

Current attention layout:

- QKV projection stays as the existing CUTLASS linear GEMM.
- QKV bias is applied in-place to the QKV scratch.
- QK uses CUTLASS `GemmBatched` per head over a fixed padded score/prob scratch.
- Softmax uses the fixed `seq_len=51` warp kernel over the score rows.
- V is packed into the same attention scratch for tensor-op alignment.
- PV uses CUTLASS `GemmBatched` per head.
- Removed transformer pointer-table scratch (`attention_q_ptrs`, `attention_k_ptrs`, score/prob/context pointer arrays); MLP Stream1 path is unchanged.

This is not a fallback path and does not change model weights or outputs.

## Verification

Docker image: `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24`.

```bash
docker run --rm --gpus all -v "${PWD}:/workspace" -w /workspace gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24 bash -lc "cmake --build build-stream1-opt --target stream1_transformer_cuda_tests stream_benchmark -j2 && ./build-stream1-opt/stream1_transformer_cuda_tests"
```

Result:

```text
stream1_transformer_cuda_tests=pass
```

## Local Benchmark

Weights: `test_results/stream1_transformer_reference/weights_fp16`.
Puzzle: `0`.

| B_MICRO | concurrency | ms/group | parents/s | candidates/s | scratch bytes | report |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 512 | 1 | 21.2628 | 24079.6 | 577909.4 | 163864576 | `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_512x1_2026-07-01.md` |
| 1024 | 1 | 41.4473 | 24706.1 | 592946.4 | 327729152 | `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_1024x1_2026-07-01.md` |
| 512 | 2 | 40.7648 | 25119.7 | 602873.7 | 327729152 | `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_512x2_2026-07-01.md` |

For comparison, the previous local `512x1` GemmArray report in this worktree showed `565930.9` candidates/s with `164126720` scratch bytes, so this clean tensor path is slightly faster locally and uses less scratch in that point.

## Notes

A first scalar warp-only rewrite was tested and rejected because it passed correctness but dropped to `51780.6` candidates/s at `512x1`. The final implementation keeps tensor-core QK/PV while removing the pointer-array attention machinery.
Additional verification after scratch struct change:

```bash
docker run --rm --gpus all -v "${PWD}:/workspace" -w /workspace gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24 bash -lc "cmake --build build-stream1-opt --target contract_tests dispatcher_cuda_tests -j2 && ./build-stream1-opt/contract_tests && ./build-stream1-opt/dispatcher_cuda_tests"
```

Result:

```text
contract_tests=pass
dispatcher_cuda_tests=pass
```

+# Stream1 Piece Transformer PyTorch Gap Analysis - 2026-07-02
+
+## Question
+
+Explain why the native Stream1 piece-transformer backend is slower than the
+PyTorch fast path on Kaggle T4, even though the native MLP backend is faster
+than its PyTorch equivalent.
+
+## Evidence
+
+### PyTorch model path
+
+The Kaggle artifact `vladkuznetsov266/megaminx-qtransformer-1782210824`
+uses the model code under:
+
+- `test_results/kaggle_model_artifact_download/megaminx-transformer/pilgrim/model.py`
+
+The fast transformer block does:
+
+1. `LayerNorm`
+2. one `F.linear(..., in_proj_weight, in_proj_bias)` for QKV
+3. reshape Q, K, V to `[batch, heads, seq, head_dim]`
+4. one `F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, dropout_p=0.0, is_causal=False)`
+5. one output `F.linear`
+6. residual add
+7. `LayerNorm`
+8. `Linear -> SiLU -> Linear`
+9. residual add
+
+The piece input fast path precomputes:
+
+- `fast_slot_projected`
+- `fast_piece_static`
+- `fast_piece_masks`
+
+So the PyTorch speed is not from a different model. It is the same folded
+piece-token construction idea plus PyTorch's optimized SDPA and linear kernels.
+
+PyTorch documents `scaled_dot_product_attention` as an efficient implementation
+which can automatically select optimized CUDA kernels, including FlashAttention
+and memory-efficient attention, when the input supports them:
+
+- https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/generated/torch.nn.functional.scaled_dot_product_attention.html
+- https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/backends.html
+
+### Native Stream1 path
+
+The current native code in `cuda/stream1.cu` still decomposes attention:
+
+1. `stream1_transformer_add_qkv_bias_kernel`
+2. `stream1_transformer_qk_batched_launch`
+   - loops over 8 heads
+   - launches CUTLASS `GemmBatched` per head
+3. `stream1_transformer_pack_v51_kernel`
+4. `stream<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="582" />ause
+
+The native transformer backend is slower because its attention is not equivalent
+to PyTorch's fused SDPA execution strategy.
+
+PyTorch pays for one high-level SDPA call per layer and lets CUDA backend
+dispatch pick a fused attention implementation when possible. Our native path
+materializes and reloads intermediate attention tensors across many kernels:
+
+- QKV bias is separate from QKV GEMM.
+- QK is launched once per head.
+- softmax is a separate global-memory pass.
+- V is repacked into scratch.
+- PV is launched once per head.
+- residual/bias and SiLU epilogues are separate kernels.
+
+The current native implementation uses tensor cores for QK/PV GEMMs, but it
+still loses on launch count, global-memory roundtrips, and per-head
+fragmentation. On T4 this overhead dominates enough that PyTorch is faster.
+
+## What To Do Next
+
+Do not add a PyTorch fallback. The native transformer backend needs a better
+attention backend.
+
+Priority order:
+
+1. Capture a bounded PyTorch profiler or Nsight trace on Kaggle T4 for exactly
+   one warmed direct-forward batch, to record actual SDPA kernel names.
+2. Add a bounded capture mode to `stream_benchmark` so Nsight profiles one
+   `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=2` group instead of the broad sweep.
+3. Replace per-head QK + softmax + V-pack + per-head PV with a native fused
+   short-SDPA backend for fixed `seq_len=51`, `head_dim=32`, `nhead=8`.
+4. Fuse epilogues where possible:
+   - QKV GEMM + bias
+   - FF1 GEMM + bias + SiLU
+   - attention output GEMM + residual + bias
+   - FF2 GEMM + residual + bias
+5. Keep the MLP path separate and unchanged.
+
+## Current Confidence
+
+High. The code-path comparison and Kaggle measurements agree. The missing
+piece is exact PyTorch CUDA kernel names on Kaggle T4, but the root cause does
+not depend on those names: PyTorch uses a single SDPA abstraction, while native
+currently decomposes attention into many launch and scratch stages.
*** End Patch

import csv

from pathlib import Path

base = Path("/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/beam8a100")
logs = base / "ihes_cube_model" / "logs"
repo = base / "cayleypy-beam-results"
out_base = repo / "data" / "ihes_cube"

skip_parts = ("_original_", "_reflected_", "_sources_", "_plan_")
files = [
    p for p in sorted(logs.glob("solve_bucket_fresh_*.tsv"))
    if not any(x in p.name for x in skip_parts) and p.stat().st_size > 200
]

fields = [
    "puzzle_id", "variant", "source_index", "known_length", "found_length",
    "delta", "run_puzzle_id", "depth_index", "found_depth", "owner_rank",
    "solution_path", "source_solution",
]

by_puzzle = {}
sources = {}

for p in files:
    with p.open(newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        reader = csv.DictReader(fh, delimiter="\t")
        for row in reader:
            pid = row.get("puzzle_id", "").strip()
            sol = row.get("solution_path", "").strip()
            if not pid or not sol:
                continue
            clean = {k: row.get(k, "") for k in fields}
            key = (
                clean["puzzle_id"], clean["variant"], clean["source_index"],
                clean["found_length"], clean["solution_path"], clean["source_solution"],
            )
            by_puzzle.setdefault(pid, {})
            by_puzzle[pid][key] = clean

# Stream1 MLP Native vs PyTorch Kaggle 2xT4 - 2026-07-01

Kaggle kernel: `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-mlp-native-vs-torch-2xt4-benchmark`, version 2.

Source tag: `stream1-mlp-torch-kaggle-v2-code` at `cc90a3d`.

Status: `KernelWorkerStatus.COMPLETE`.

## Best Per-GPU Throughput

| GPU | Native B_MICRO | Native concurrency | Native candidates/s | Torch rows | Torch candidates/s | Native / Torch |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 4096 | 1 | 16,416,335.8 | 16,384 | 8,133,621.3 | 2.0183x |
| 1 | 4096 | 1 | 15,728,371.2 | 4,096 | 6,763,818.6 | 2.3254x |

Aggregate best throughput across both T4 GPUs:

- Native CUTLASS MLP: 32,144,707.0 candidates/s.
- PyTorch preindexed MLP: 14,897,439.9 candidates/s.
- Native / Torch aggregate: 2.1577x.

## Evidence Files

Downloaded Kaggle outputs are under:

- `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_benchmark_v2_output/mlp_native_vs_torch_comparison.csv`
- `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_benchmark_v2_output/native_mlp_benchmark_rows.csv`
- `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_benchmark_v2_output/torch_mlp_benchmark_rows.csv`
- `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_benchmark_v2_output/cayley-beam-mlp-native-vs-torch-2xt4-benchmark.log`

## Interpretation

For the exported folded MLP weights, the native CUTLASS Stream1 path is about 2.0-2.3x faster than a direct PyTorch implementation on Kaggle T4. This establishes the local comparison baseline for judging the transformer backend: the transformer PyTorch fast path being faster than native means the transformer native path still has implementation overhead, not that native CUDA is inherently slower than Torch for this project.

# Stream1 Piece Transformer Clean Tensor Attention - 2026-07-01

## Change

Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path away from the previous pointer-array `GemmArray` implementation.

Current attention layout:

- QKV projection stays as the existing CUTLASS linear GEMM.
- QKV bias is applied in-place to the QKV scratch.
- QK uses CUTLASS `GemmBatched` per head over a fixed padded score/prob scratch.
- Softmax uses the fixed `seq_len=51` warp kernel over the score rows.
- V is packed into the same attention scratch for tensor-op alignment.
- PV uses CUTLASS `GemmBatched` per head.
- Removed transformer pointer-table scratch (`attention_q_ptrs`, `attention_k_ptrs`, score/prob/context pointer arrays); MLP Stream1 path is unchanged.

This is not a fallback path and does not change model weights or outputs.

## Verification

Docker image: `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24`.

```bash
docker run --rm --gpus all -v "${PWD}:/workspace" -w /workspace gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24 bash -lc "cmake --build build-stream1-opt --target stream1_transformer_cuda_tests stream_benchmark -j2 && ./build-stream1-opt/stream1_transformer_cuda_tests"
```

Result:

```text
stream1_transformer_cuda_tests=pass
```

## Local Benchmark

Weights: `test_results/stream1_transformer_reference/weights_fp16`.
Puzzle: `0`.

| B_MICRO | concurrency | ms/group | parents/s | candidates/s | scratch bytes | report |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 512 | 1 | 21.2628 | 24079.6 | 577909.4 | 163864576 | `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_512x1_2026-07-01.md` |
| 1024 | 1 | 41.4473 | 24706.1 | 592946.4 | 327729152 | `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_1024x1_2026-07-01.md` |
| 512 | 2 | 40.7648 | 25119.7 | 602873.7 | 327729152 | `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_512x2_2026-07-01.md` |

For comparison, the previous local `512x1` GemmArray report in this worktree showed `565930.9` candidates/s with `164126720` scratch bytes, so this clean tensor path is slightly faster locally and uses less scratch in that point.

## Notes

A first scalar warp-only rewrite was tested and rejected because it passed correctness but dropped to `51780.6` candidates/s at `512x1`. The final implementation keeps tensor-core QK/PV while removing the pointer-array attention machinery.

+# Stream1 Piece Transformer PyTorch Gap Analysis - 2026-07-02
+
+## Question
+
+Explain why the native Stream1 piece-transformer backend is slower than the
+PyTorch fast path on Kaggle T4, even though the native MLP backend is faster
+than its PyTorch equivalent.
+
+## Evidence
+
+### PyTorch model path
+
+The Kaggle artifact `vladkuznetsov266/megaminx-qtransformer-1782210824`
+uses the model code under:
+
+- `test_results/kaggle_model_artifact_download/megaminx-transformer/pilgrim/model.py`
+
+The fast transformer block does:
+
+1. `LayerNorm`
+2. one `F.linear(..., in_proj_weight, in_proj_bias)` for QKV
+3. reshape Q, K, V to `[batch, heads, seq, head_dim]`
+4. one `F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, dropout_p=0.0, is_causal=False)`
+5. one output `F.linear`
+6. residual add
+7. `LayerNorm`
+8. `Linear -> SiLU -> Linear`
+9. residual add
+
+The piece input fast path precomputes:
+
+- `fast_slot_projected`
+- `fast_piece_static`
+- `fast_piece_masks`
+
+So the PyTorch speed is not from a different model. It is the same folded
+piece-token construction idea plus PyTorch's optimized SDPA and linear kernels.
+
+PyTorch documents `scaled_dot_product_attention` as an efficient implementation
+which can automatically select optimized CUDA kernels, including FlashAttention
+and memory-efficient attention, when the input supports them:
+
+- https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/generated/torch.nn.functional.scaled_dot_product_attention.html
+- https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/backends.html
+
+### Native Stream1 path
+
+The current native code in `cuda/stream1.cu` still decomposes attention:
+
+1. `stream1_transformer_add_qkv_bias_kernel`
+2. `stream1_transformer_qk_batched_launch`
+   - loops over 8 heads
+   - launches CUTLASS `GemmBatched` per head
+3. `stream1_transformer_pack_v51_kernel`
+4. `stream<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="582" />ause
+
+The native transformer backend is slower because its attention is not equivalent
+to PyTorch's fused SDPA execution strategy.
+
+PyTorch pays for one high-level SDPA call per layer and lets CUDA backend
+dispatch pick a fused attention implementation when possible. Our native path
+materializes and reloads intermediate attention tensors across many kernels:
+
+- QKV bias is separate from QKV GEMM.
+- QK is launched once per head.
+- softmax is a separate global-memory pass.
+- V is repacked into scratch.
+- PV is launched once per head.
+- residual/bias and SiLU epilogues are separate kernels.
+
+The current native implementation uses tensor cores for QK/PV GEMMs, but it
+still loses on launch count, global-memory roundtrips, and per-head
+fragmentation. On T4 this overhead dominates enough that PyTorch is faster.
+
+## What To Do Next
+
+Do not add a PyTorch fallback. The native transformer backend needs a better
+attention backend.
+
+Priority order:
+
+1. Capture a bounded PyTorch profiler or Nsight trace on Kaggle T4 for exactly
+   one warmed direct-forward batch, to record actual SDPA kernel names.
+2. Add a bounded capture mode to `stream_benchmark` so Nsight profiles one
+   `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=2` group instead of the broad sweep.
+3. Replace per-head QK + softmax + V-pack + per-head PV with a native fused
+   short-SDPA backend for fixed `seq_len=51`, `head_dim=32`, `nhead=8`.
+4. Fuse epilogues where possible:
+   - QKV GEMM + bias
+   - FF1 GEMM + bias + SiLU
+   - attention output GEMM + residual + bias
+   - FF2 GEMM + residual + bias
+5. Keep the MLP path separate and unchanged.
+
+## Current Confidence
+
+High. The code-path comparison and Kaggle measurements agree. The missing
+piece is exact PyTorch CUDA kernel names on Kaggle T4, but the root cause does
+not depend on those names: PyTorch uses a single SDPA abstraction, while native
+currently decomposes attention into many launch and scratch stages.
*** End Patch

# Stream1 Piece Transformer PyTorch Gap Analysis - 2026-07-02

## Question

Explain why the native Stream1 piece-transformer backend is slower than the
PyTorch fast path on Kaggle T4, even though the native MLP backend is faster
than its PyTorch equivalent.

## Evidence

### PyTorch model path

The Kaggle artifact `vladkuznetsov266/megaminx-qtransformer-1782210824`
uses the model code under:

- `test_results/kaggle_model_artifact_download/megaminx-transformer/pilgrim/model.py`

The fast transformer block does:

1. `LayerNorm`
2. one `F.linear(..., in_proj_weight, in_proj_bias)` for QKV
3. reshape Q, K, V to `[batch, heads, seq, head_dim]`
4. one `F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, dropout_p=0.0, is_causal=False)`
5. one output `F.linear`
6. residual add
7. `LayerNorm`
8. `Linear -> SiLU -> Linear`
9. residual add

The piece input fast path precomputes:

- `fast_slot_projected`
- `fast_piece_static`
- `fast_piece_masks`

So the PyTorch speed is not from a different model. It is the same folded
piece-token construction idea plus PyTorch's optimized SDPA and linear kernels.

PyTorch documents `scaled_dot_product_attention` as an efficient implementation
which can automatically select optimized CUDA kernels, including FlashAttention
and memory-efficient attention, when the input supports them:

- https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/generated/torch.nn.functional.scaled_dot_product_attention.html
- https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/backends.html

### Native Stream1 path

The current native code in `cuda/stream1.cu` still decomposes attention:

1. `stream1_transformer_add_qkv_bias_kernel`
2. `stream1_transformer_qk_batched_launch`
   - loops over 8 heads
   - launches CUTLASS `GemmBatched` per head
3. `stream1_transformer_pack_v51_kernel`
4. `stream1_transformer_softmax51_kernel`
5. `stream1_transformer_pv_batched_launch`
   - loops over 8 head<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="525" />is specific to the transformer native backend.

## Root Cause

The native transformer backend is slower because its attention is not equivalent
to PyTorch's fused SDPA execution strategy.

PyTorch pays for one high-level SDPA call per layer and lets CUDA backend
dispatch pick a fused attention implementation when possible. Our native path
materializes and reloads intermediate attention tensors across many kernels:

- QKV bias is separate from QKV GEMM.
- QK is launched once per head.
- softmax is a separate global-memory pass.
- V is repacked into scratch.
- PV is launched once per head.
- residual/bias and SiLU epilogues are separate kernels.

The current native implementation uses tensor cores for QK/PV GEMMs, but it
still loses on launch count, global-memory roundtrips, and per-head
fragmentation. On T4 this overhead dominates enough that PyTorch is faster.

## What To Do Next

Do not add a PyTorch fallback. The native transformer backend needs a better
attention backend.

Priority order:

1. Capture a bounded PyTorch profiler or Nsight trace on Kaggle T4 for exactly
   one warmed direct-forward batch, to record actual SDPA kernel names.
2. Add a bounded capture mode to `stream_benchmark` so Nsight profiles one
   `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=2` group instead of the broad sweep.
3. Replace per-head QK + softmax + V-pack + per-head PV with a native fused
   short-SDPA backend for fixed `seq_len=51`, `head_dim=32`, `nhead=8`.
4. Fuse epilogues where possible:
   - QKV GEMM + bias
   - FF1 GEMM + bias + SiLU
   - attention output GEMM + residual + bias
   - FF2 GEMM + residual + bias
5. Keep the MLP path separate and unchanged.

## Current Confidence

High. The code-path comparison and Kaggle measurements agree. The missing
piece is exact PyTorch CUDA kernel names on Kaggle T4, but the root cause does
not depend on those names: PyTorch uses a single SDPA abstraction, while native
currently decomposes attention into many launch and scratch stages.

# Stream1 Piece Transformer PyTorch Gap Analysis - 2026-07-02

## Question

Explain why the native Stream1 piece-transformer backend is slower than the
PyTorch fast path on Kaggle T4, even though the native MLP backend is faster
than its PyTorch equivalent.

## Evidence

### PyTorch model path

The Kaggle artifact `vladkuznetsov266/megaminx-qtransformer-1782210824`
uses the model code under:

- `test_results/kaggle_model_artifact_download/megaminx-transformer/pilgrim/model.py`

The fast transformer block does:

1. `LayerNorm`
2. one `F.linear(..., in_proj_weight, in_proj_bias)` for QKV
3. reshape Q, K, V to `[batch, heads, seq, head_dim]`
4. one `F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, dropout_p=0.0, is_causal=False)`
5. one output `F.linear`
6. residual add
7. `LayerNorm`
8. `Linear -> SiLU -> Linear`
9. residual add

The piece input fast path precomputes:

- `fast_slot_projected`
- `fast_piece_static`
- `fast_piece_masks`

So the PyTorch speed is not from a different model. It is the same folded
piece-token construction idea plus PyTorch's optimized SDPA and linear kernels.

PyTorch documents `scaled_dot_product_attention` as an efficient implementation
which can automatically select optimized CUDA kernels, including FlashAttention
and memory-efficient attention, when the input supports them:

- https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/generated/torch.nn.functional.scaled_dot_product_attention.html
- https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/2.12/backends.html

### Native Stream1 path

The current native code in `cuda/stream1.cu` still decomposes attention:

1. `stream1_transformer_add_qkv_bias_kernel`
2. `stream1_transformer_qk_batched_launch`
   - loops over 8 heads
   - launches CUTLASS `GemmBatched` per head
3. `stream1_transformer_pack_v51_kernel`
4. `stream1_transformer_softmax51_kernel`
5. `stream1_transformer_pv_batched_launch`
   - loops over 8 head<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="525" />is specific to the transformer native backend.

## Root Cause

The native transformer backend is slower because its attention is not equivalent
to PyTorch's fused SDPA execution strategy.

PyTorch pays for one high-level SDPA call per layer and lets CUDA backend
dispatch pick a fused attention implementation when possible. Our native path
materializes and reloads intermediate attention tensors across many kernels:

- QKV bias is separate from QKV GEMM.
- QK is launched once per head.
- softmax is a separate global-memory pass.
- V is repacked into scratch.
- PV is launched once per head.
- residual/bias and SiLU epilogues are separate kernels.

The current native implementation uses tensor cores for QK/PV GEMMs, but it
still loses on launch count, global-memory roundtrips, and per-head
fragmentation. On T4 this overhead dominates enough that PyTorch is faster.

## What To Do Next

Do not add a PyTorch fallback. The native transformer backend needs a better
attention backend.

Priority order:

1. Capture a bounded PyTorch profiler or Nsight trace on Kaggle T4 for exactly
   one warmed direct-forward batch, to record actual SDPA kernel names.
2. Add a bounded capture mode to `stream_benchmark` so Nsight profiles one
   `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=2` group instead of the broad sweep.
3. Replace per-head QK + softmax + V-pack + per-head PV with a native fused
   short-SDPA backend for fixed `seq_len=51`, `head_dim=32`, `nhead=8`.
4. Fuse epilogues where possible:
   - QKV GEMM + bias
   - FF1 GEMM + bias + SiLU
   - attention output GEMM + residual + bias
   - FF2 GEMM + residual + bias
5. Keep the MLP path separate and unchanged.

## Current Confidence

High. The code-path comparison and Kaggle measurements agree. The missing
piece is exact PyTorch CUDA kernel names on Kaggle T4, but the root cause does
not depend on those names: PyTorch uses a single SDPA abstraction, while native
currently decomposes attention into many launch and scratch stages.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
from __future__ import annotations

import csv
import json
import os
import sys
import time
from pathlib import Path

import torch


INPUT = Path("/kaggle/input")
WORK = Path("/kaggle/working")
MODEL_ARTIFACT_NAME = "megaminx_qtransformer_1782210824_e99997"
MODEL_ID = 1782210824
GROUP_ID = 900
TARGET_ID = 0
OUTPUT_CSV = WORK / "transformer_inference_speed.csv"
OUTPUT_JSON = WORK / "transformer_inference_speed_summary.json"


def find_model_root() -> Path:
    candidates = []
    for manifest in INPUT.rglob("manifest.json"):
        try:
            data = json.loads(manifest.read_text())
        except Exception:
            continue
        if data.get("name") == MODEL_ARTIFACT_NAME:
            candidates.append(manifest.parent)
    if not candidates:
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Add the Kaggle Model artifact {MODEL_ARTIFACT_NAME}.")
    return candidates[0] / "megaminx-transformer"


MODEL_ROOT = find_model_root()
if str(MODEL_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(MODEL_ROOT))

from pilgrim import build_model_from_info, configure_inference_backend, generate_inverse_moves, parse_generator_spec  # noqa: E402
from pilgrim.model import batch_process  # noqa: E402
from pilgrim.utils import load_torch_file  # noqa: E402


def model_info() -> tuple[Path, dict]:
    base = f"model_p{GROUP_ID:03d}-t{TARGET_ID:03d}"
    hits = sorted((MODEL_ROOT / "logs").glob(f"{base}*_{MODEL_ID}.json"))
    if len(hits) != 1:
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"expected one metadata file for model_id={MODEL_ID}, got {hits}")
    return hits[0], json.loads(hits[0].read_text(encoding="utf-8"))


def is_q(info: dict) -> bool:
    return str(info.get("model_name", "")).endswith("-q") or str(info.get("training_mode", "")).startswith("q_")

int(event.count),\n+                \"self_cuda_us\": cuda_us,\n+                \"cuda_total_us\": total_cuda_us,\n+                \"self_cuda_pct\": 0.0,\n+            }\n+        )\n+    total_self_cuda = sum(float(row[\"self_cuda_us\"]) for row in rows)\n+    for row in rows:\n+        row[\"self_cuda_pct\"] = 100.0 * float(row[\"self_cuda_us\"]) / total_self_cuda if total_self_cuda > 0.0 else 0.0\n+    rows.sort(key=lambda row: float(row[\"self_cuda_us\"]), reverse=True)\n+\n+    with OUTPUT_CSV.open(\"w\", newline=\"\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n+        writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=[\"key\", \"count\", \"self_cuda_us\", \"cuda_total_us\", \"self_cuda_pct\"])\n+        writer.writeheader()\n+        writer.writerows(rows)\n+\n+    sample = float(out.reshape(-1)[0].detach().float().item())\n+    peak_gib = torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated() / (1024.0**3)\n+    lines = [\n+        \"# Transformer Inference PyTorch Profile\",\n+        \"\",\n+        f\"- torch={torch.__version__}\",\n+        f\"- cuda={torch.version.cuda}\",\n+        f\"- device={torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)}\",\n+        f\"- backend={backend}\",\n+        f\"- output_dim={output_dim}\",\n+        f\"- batch={batch}\",\n+        f\"- warmup={warmup}\",\n+        f\"- active_iters={active}\",\n+        f\"- peak_mem_gib={peak_gib:.3f}\",\n+        f\"- metadata={info_path}\",\n+        f\"- weights={weights}\",\n+        f\"- sample={sample}\",\n+        \"\",\n+        \"| rank | key | count | self CUDA us | CUDA total us | self CUDA pct |\",\n+        \"|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\",\n+    ]\n+    for rank, row in enumerate(rows[:40], start=1):\n+        lines.append(\n+            \"| {rank} | `{key}` | {count} | {self_cuda_us:.3f} | {cuda_total_us:.3f} | {self_cuda_pct:.2f} |\".format(\n+                rank=rank,\n+                **row,\n+            )\n+        )\n+    OUTPUT_MD.write_text(\"\\n\".join(lines) + \"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+\n+    print(\n+        \"KAGGLE_TORCH_PROFILE_DONE\"\n+        f\" batch={batch}\"\n+        f\" active_iters={active}\"\n+        f\" rows={len(rows)}\"\n+        f\" peak_mem_gib={peak_gib:.3f}\"\n+        f\" output_csv={OUTPUT_CSV}\"\n+        f\" output_md={OUTPUT_MD}\",\n+        flush=True,\n+    )\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    main()\n*** End - Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention hot path to use CUTLASS tensor-core QK/PV attention instead of the scalar/warp attention kernel. The current execution path uses device pointer-array scratch plus `GemmArray` QK/PV launches, padded half score/prob/V scratch, and chunked attention batches so `B_MICRO=8192` no longer fails at `8192*8=65536` attention matrices. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; best local T4 sweep point is `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1` at `583142.3` candidates/s. Nsight now shows the remaining bottleneck is mostly linear GEMMs and unfused elementwise transformer kernels rather than the old attention kernel. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_tensor_attention_rewrite_2026-06-30.md`.
- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path into a clean tensor-core implementation without pointer-array scratch or CUTLASS `GemmArray`. The new path keeps QKV/FF/output GEMMs unchanged, applies QKV bias in-place, uses per-head CUTLASS `GemmBatched` QK/PV with one padded score/prob/V scratch, and preserves the MLP Stream1 path. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; local fp16 benchmark points reached `577909.4` candidates/s at `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1`, `592946.4` at `1024x1`, and `602873.7` at `512x2`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_2026-07-01.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 5 against commit `78052c5` with an explicit notebook commit check. Kaggle completed successfully, but clean tensor attention only reached `821205.4` aggregate candidates/s (`406086.6` on GPU0, `415118.8` on GPU1), about `1.01x` v4 and still only `0.64-0.66x` of the PyTorch `batch_process` fast path per T4. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v5_2026-07-01.md`.

- Fixed the Torch-side Stream1 MLP benchmark parser to accept `initial_state_id` from `data/test.csv`, matching the native benchmark input schema. Kaggle 2xT4 v1 had completed native CUTLASS MLP measurements but failed before Torch timing because the parser only accepted `id/puzzle_id/ID`. Evidence: `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_native_vs_torch_v1_failure_2026-07-01.md`.

Best native CUTLASS MLP reached `16.42M` candidates/s on GPU0 and `15.73M` on GPU1; best PyTorch preindexed MLP reached `8.13M` and `6.76M` candidates/s. Native is `2.02x`/`2.33x` faster per GPU and `2.16x` faster in aggregate. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_mlp_native_vs_torch_kaggle_v2_2026-07-01.md`.

The analysis identifies native attention decomposition, per-head QK/PV launches, separate softmax/V-pack, and unfused epilogues as the current T4 performance gap; no solver runtime or MLP path behavior changed.

- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention hot path to use CUTLASS tensor-core QK/PV attention instead of the scalar/warp attention kernel. The current execution path uses device pointer-array scratch plus `GemmArray` QK/PV launches, padded half score/prob/V scratch, and chunked attention batches so `B_MICRO=8192` no longer fails at `8192*8=65536` attention matrices. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; best local T4 sweep point is `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1` at `583142.3` candidates/s. Nsight now shows the remaining bottleneck is mostly linear GEMMs and unfused elementwise transformer kernels rather than the old attention kernel. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_tensor_attention_rewrite_2026-06-30.md`.
- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path into a clean tensor-core implementation without pointer-array scratch or CUTLASS `GemmArray`. The new path keeps QKV/FF/output GEMMs unchanged, applies QKV bias in-place, uses per-head CUTLASS `GemmBatched` QK/PV with one padded score/prob/V scratch, and preserves the MLP Stream1 path. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v5_2026-07-01.md`.
